## MS FILOMENA BARROS DOS REIS (TLS) AAA President’s Message 

## Dear Readers, 

## I welcome you all to the beautiful city of Yecheon, South Korea, for the 20 th Asian U20 Athletics 

## Championships. 

## It is with immense pleasure that we introduce to you our latest publication, a captivating 

## statistics book that dives into the world of U20 athletic records. 

## This comprehensive guide showcases the relentless pursuit of excellence and unwavering 

## determination. From breathtaking sprints to awe-inspiring jumps, from record-breaking 

## throws to elegant vaults, this book captures the essence of youthful vigor and raw talent that 

## fuels the spirit of these remarkable athletes. 

## As we come together to celebrate the remarkable athletes competing in the 20 th Asian U20 

## Athletics Championships, we invite you to embrace this book as a testament to the dedication, 

## perseverance, and extraordinary abilities of our champions. May it serve as a source of 

## inspiration and a medium for future generations of athletes to push beyond boundaries and 

## redefine what is possible. 

## I would like to extend my heartfelt gratitude to the Editor and Statisticians who have 

## meticulously compiled and curated this invaluable collection. 

## May this book ignite a deep appreciation for the U20 track and field records and inspire you 

## to witness the immense talent that will grace the tracks and fields of Yecheon. 

## Sincerely, 

## Gen. Dahlan Al Hamad 

## President 

## Asian Athletics Association Welcome to Yecheon! 

# We sincerely welcome all the athletes, officials, and distinguished guests from 

# home and abroad to the "2023 Yecheon Asian U20 Athletics Championships." 

# As U20 athletes with great talents and skills from Asia gather together, we are 

# delighted to host the 20th Asian U20 Athletics Championships in Yecheon, a sports 

# city where history, culture, friendship, and competition blend harmoniously for 

# this festive occasion. 

# I express my deepest gratitude to President Dhalan Al Hamad and the members 

# of AAA council, who have provided us with great trust and support, enabling 

# Yecheon to host a successful Asian U20 Athletics Championships, five years after 

# the previous edition. 

# With participants from 27 Asian countries, including over 1,500 participating team 

# members and competition officials, we are confident that this event will be the 

# highest level of competition ever held. 

# We sincerely wish all the participants of the "2023 Yecheon Asian U20 Athletics 

# Championships" a successful and fruitful experience during their visit to Yecheon, 

# Republic of Korea. 

# Hyunpyo Yook

# Korea Association of Athletics Federations Asian Junior (U20) Athletics Championships STATISTICS HANDBOOK Yecheon 2023 

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

## Editor’s Note & Acknowledgments 

This Statistics Handbook is a work hugely inspired by various Statistical compendiums published by the 

World Athletics and excellently edited by my long-time friend Mark Butler for the past several years. 

Practicing and participating in competitive athletics and other sports commence at a young age. A

sportsperson evolves into a superstar after taking part in school and collegiate sporting competitions at 

the beginning. 

National and regional championships and athletics matches include separate events for boys and girls 

for the past several decades. It helps to groom the club culture, as also to identify and nurture the 

talented ones from their young ages. 

The IAAF, after the successful introduction of its own World championships in Helsinki in 1983, realised 

the importance of having a separate meet for the talented juniors. Athens, the mecca of the Olympic 

movement that is said to have invented a large form of athletic contests in time immemorial, has come 

forward to host the inaugural edition of the World Junior Championships in 1986. Initially, competitions 

for boys under 20 years and girls under 19 years took place in that championship. Later, in order to 

give an equal opportunity to the junior women, girls under 20 years of age are allowed and thus the 

biennial meet was rechristened as “World Athletics U20 Championships”. 

Prompted by the introduction of a separate global competition for the juniors, the Asian Athletics 

Association immediately launched the Asian Junior Championships in the same year and the first edition 

of it was successfully organised in Jakarta, Indonesia, from December 4 to 7, 1986. 

Yecheon, a picturesque Korean town, is organising the 20 th edition of the Asian U20 championships 

from June 4 to 7, 2023. It is the first time that South Korea is hosting this prestigious championship. 

Incidentally this championship is being organised after a five years gap as Japanese city of Gifu hosted 

the previous edition in 2018. Like many other sporting events affected by Covid-19 pandemic, the 

2020 edition of the Asian U20 championships planned in the summer of 2020 in Bangkok, also suffered 

a cancellation. 

The Asian Athletics Association resumed its competitions in 2022 with a memorable Asian U18 

championships in Kuwait and is ably managing to execute a crowded competition calendar in 2023. 

With a large number of enthusiastic athletes representing the major sporting nations of the continent 

expected to vie for honours in Yecheon, we decided to present this Statistics Handbook, a third of such 

publication this year after the previous ones for the Asian indoor championships in Astana and the Asian 

U18 championships in Tashkent, to everyone concerned to follow the contests in the Korean city in a 

diligent manner. 

In this handbook, you may find a List of Past Medallists, Best National Placings per event, National U20 

Records or Best Performances, Progression of Asian U20 Records, and the Asian All-Time Top-20 

Lists for the said age group. 

List of Past Medallists: 

In this section, you may find the list of all medallists in all events conducted during the past 18 editions 

of the championships held so far. When there was a doping disqualification, the result was adjusted by 

upgrading the next-placed athletes to the medals lists. “^” indicates such changes in the respective 

year of competition for the concerned event. Asian Junior (U20) Athletics Championships STATISTICS HANDBOOK Yecheon 2023 

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

Best National Placings: 

This section gives information about the best order of finish (in finals or qualifying rounds, as the case 

may be) for each nation in each event. Here again, the “ ^” symbol shows the change/upgrade in the 

order of finish following the disqualification of an athlete concerned. 

National U20 Records: 

The best-known performance per event per nation is included if a country does not have its own national 

U20 records. However, wherever a country is having such records but does not recognise a particular 

performance the same is mentioned with a “ *” after the country code for the respective event. Additional 

notes (#) can be found at the bottom of each event where a superior mark is not recognised by the 

respective national federation for various reasons. In any track event “ +” shows an intermediate mark 

en route to a longer distance. Manual (hand) timings in track events are indicated with “ m” after such 

performance. The Central Asian republics (KAZ, KGZ, TJK, TKM, UZB) recognised their athletes’ 

performances from the Soviet Era. Many such marks were superior to the listed Asian U20 Records. 

However, those marks are considered only in this section as per the interest of the respective members. 

Progression of Asian U20 Records: 

In this section, we have listed the evolution of the best Asian U20 marks from 1999 onwards. However, 

in some strong events, we have included the records created prior to that year. With variations in the 

weight of the implements used in throwing events and the height of the hurdles in recent years, we have 

mentioned the details in respective events. 

Asian All-Time U20 Top Lists: 

You may find in this section the Top-20 Performers (athletes) in each event. Wherever the majority of 

the list is dominated by athletes from China and Japan (or any other country), additional top athletes 

from other countries are included in the respective events keeping in mind at least athletes from a 

minimum of five countries and territories appeared in it. My friend and ATFS member Paco Ascorbe 

dedicated his valuable time for compiling the All-Time Lists. 

Acknowledgments: 

I principally acknowledge the support received from Heinrich Hubbeling , the Asian AA Statistician, 

who for the past 35 years compiled the annual and all-time Asian Ranking Lists. A great deal of 

information was provided by Mirko Jalava ( Tilastopaja ), Michel Saint-Raymond ( AthleRecords ), Sevket 

F. Erbay ( Athletics Podium ), Peter Matthews ( Athletics International ), Winfried Kramer ( National 

Records for All Countries in the World ), Alfonz Juck ( EME News ), David Monti ( Race Results Weekly ), 

Marco Buccellato (Atletica), Tony Hunt (World Junior Athletics), Michel Naze and Stephen Sprague. 

I also fondly remembered the great work by the late Lionel Peters in collating the junior and youth 

athletics statistics for so many years, which were still forming a base for anyone working on both U20 

and U18 categories. 

Mention must be made to Paco Ascorbe , who spent his valuable time putting in his efforts to create 

the deep Asian All-Time Lists. 

Mark Butler, Richard Hymans, Paul Jenes, and Felix Capilla for their inspiration and encouragement 

from time to time. 

I would like to thank Rahul D Pawar for coordinating between various championship organisers of the 

past and AAA Statistician Heinrich Hubbeling in compiling the edition-wise results service. 

Many of my friends associated with the World Athletics supported me in my work on various occasions. 

For that, I would like to thank Ottavio Castellini, Sean Wallace-Jones, Chris Turner, Anna Legnani, 

Laura Arcoleo, Imre Matrahazi, Carlo de Angeli, Jessica Whittington, Bob Ramsak and Jon Mulkeen. Asian Junior (U20) Athletics Championships STATISTICS HANDBOOK Yecheon 2023 

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

Association of Track and Field Statisticians, in which I am a member since 1982, for moulding me into 

who I am today. 

Last but not least, to the Asian Athletics Association, especially to its President Gen. Dahlan Al Hamad 

and Secretary-General Mr A. Shuggumarran for having permitted me to produce and edit this statistics 

handbook on behalf of the AAA. 

A work of this nature takes a great deal of time and energy, and hence errors are likely to creep in. I

shall be highly thankful if you could send in any additions, corrections and comments to my email ID 

sportsmurali@gmail.com and/or sec@asianathleticsassociation.org 

I am confident that this handbook will be useful to the fans, media and officials in following the 20th 

Asian Junior (U20) Athletics Championships. 

I take this opportunity to wish all participants, officials and the organisers every success. 

Ram. Murali Krishnan

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

BEST NATIONAL PLACINGS 

This section shows the best position achieved 

by each country in every event in the Asian 

Junior Athletics Championships held so far. 

Azerbaijan (AZE), the former Soviet Republic, who was a 

member of the AAA after the disintegration of the USSR, 

took part in the Asian Junior Championships and won a 

couple of medals. However, they, later on, moved to join 

the European Athletics.

## Asian & National Junior Records

CR = Asian Junior Championship Records 

National Junior Records (or Best Performances) 

have appeared against the respective Country

## 100m:

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

## Progression of Asian U20 Records 

This list shows the evolution of Asian Junior (U20) Records (or Best Performances) from 1993 when 

the Central Asian Republics (KAZ, KGZ, TJK, TKM, UZB) joined the Asian Athletics Association. 

However, in some strong events where the records created earlier by athletes from other Asian nations 

were relevant, the same has been included. Compiled with major inputs from Mirko Jalava, Heinrich 

Hubbeling, Michel Saint-Raymond and members of the Association of Track and Field Statisticians.

## AAA Golden Jubilee Year (1973-2023) 

## Asian U20 All-Time Top Lists 

## This list is principally compiled by Paco Ascorbe (ATFS). The marks in italics show the athletes took 

## part in the former Soviet Union (URS) until 1991 and CIS in 1992. Athletes from many Central Asian 

## Republics had their best performances during the Soviet Era. However, we leave it up to the readers 

## to form their own opinions on how to value such marks. (As of 25 May 2023 )

NOTES: 

- Championships events compiled. 

- Top-20 performers + additional marks for athletes from countries which are not appeared in the main list per event. 

- Indoor performances are included in the field events, with their outdoor bests added wherever available. 

- Wind assisted performances are not considered. 

- Hand-timed performances only for distances from 1500m onwards. 

- Not confirmed performances, doubtful DOB and drug dq at the end of each list (to decide if they have to be published) 

- Performances with senior hurdles/implements only if superior to those with Junior ones 

- Performances with old model of Javelin for women (prior to April 30 th 1999) mixed in the main list 

(it affects to the JT and Heptathlon events) 

- Relays: only the best performance for each country (in fact it is almost a list of NRs). 

- In italics, performances by athletes from former USSR (until 1991) and CIS (1992) Asian Republics, and affiliated to clubs of 

those republics. 

- Competition abbreviations for global, area and national champs and games.
